invocation, not in-script timers).
"""Parametric definition of the Gridfinity USB Cablemanager (single solid).

Measured from the source STEP via introspect.py (1 solid,
reconstruct_coverage_pct=100, freeform_area_pct=0, wrap_regions=0,
boolean_risk=low) plus a raw BRepAdaptor_Surface probe (introspect's
holes/blend_features clusters do not carry a Y-location or per-region
extent - see spec.md and pitfalls.md for how the probe was built and what
it found).

Axis convention: the SOURCE STEP uses Y as the vertical axis (X = long
grid axis, 126mm / 3 gridfinity units; Z = the 42mm/1-unit depth axis).
Reproduced as-is (not remapped to Z-up) so metrics.py's raw-vector
centre-of-mass comparison lands correctly - same convention the
gridfinity-plain-bin sibling recovery uses.

Shape (per the probe): a 3x1 gridfinity-unit open-top tray, 6 equal-width
compartments (2 half-unit-pitch slots per unit) divided by 1.2mm walls,
sitting on 3 separate per-unit foot pads (a small notch relief between
units at the very bottom - the standard Gridfinity captive-foot
isolation). 12x blind d6.5 magnet press-fit pockets at the standard 26mm-
pitch/8mm-inset gridfinity grid, one set of 4 per unit.

introspect.py's holes list also reports 12 MORE d3.0-ish cylindrical faces
at a 3.9mm unit-boundary inset. A point-classification probe against the
source (BRepClass3d_SolidClassifier at several XZ positions, sweeping Y)
disproved two hypotheses in turn: that these continue the magnet hole as
a screw clearance bore (the source is solid there, no void), and that
they are an independent "screw-together" through-hole at the 3.9mm-inset
grid (also solid throughout). Both are NOT modelled here - see spec.md's
Assumptions and pitfalls.md for the dead-end investigation.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ---- Gridfinity footprint (X = long axis, Z = depth axis) ----
    units_x: int = 3                  # gridfinity units along the long (X) axis
    unit_size: float = 42.0           # mm per gridfinity grid unit
    depth_z: float = 42.0             # mm, footprint depth (Z), 1 gridfinity unit

    # ---- Outer envelope ----
    outer_corner_r: float = 4.0       # main-body outer corner fillet radius
    front_back_wall_t: float = 1.2    # front/back (Z-normal) outer wall thickness

    # ---- Compartments (the cable-routing comb) ----
    comps_per_unit: int = 2           # half-unit-pitch compartments per gridfinity unit
    wall_t: float = 1.2               # divider / end-wall thickness (X-normal walls)
    comp_corner_r: float = 4.0        # compartment interior vertical corner fillet

    # ---- Vertical bands (local h=0 at the true bottom of the foot pads) ----
    # The outer envelope's footprint TAPERS in the foot band (a 3-sub-band
    # chamfer/straight/chamfer profile, same technique as the
    # gridfinity-plain-bin sibling recovery's stacking foot): the corner
    # arc CENTRE stays fixed while its radius shrinks going down, so the
    # whole rounded-rect silhouette insets by (outer_corner_r - r) on
    # every side at that height.
    foot_height: float = 4.75         # foot/base-slab band: h=0 .. foot_height
                                       # (contains the magnet pocket;
                                       # the compartment floor sits at h=foot_height)
    foot_r0: float = 0.8              # corner radius at h=0 (the true bottom)
    foot_chamfer1_h: float = 0.8       # h=0 -> this: chamfer r: foot_r0 -> foot_r1
    foot_r1: float = 1.6
    foot_straight_h: float = 1.8       # then a straight band at r=foot_r1
    # last band (foot_chamfer1_h+foot_straight_h -> foot_height) chamfers
    # r: foot_r1 -> outer_corner_r, reaching the full envelope at foot_height
    wall_height: float = 50.0         # compartment/divider wall band, above the foot
    # total_height = foot_height + wall_height (derived)

    # ---- Per-unit foot notch (captive-foot isolation between units) ----
    notch_half_width: float = 3.2     # notch half-width at each INTERNAL unit boundary
    notch_height: float = 2.6         # notch relief height, measured up from h=0

    # ---- Magnet holes (standard gridfinity grid: 26mm pitch, 8mm inset
    #      from each unit's edges), 4 per unit, blind pocket only (see the
    #      class docstring - no screw bore continues above it) ----
    magnet_inset: float = 8.0         # hole centre inset from each unit edge (X and Z)
    magnet_hole_d: float = 6.5
    magnet_hole_depth: float = 2.6    # blind pocket, from h=0 (the true bottom)

    @property
    def total_width_x(self) -> float:
        """Full outer footprint length, X axis."""
        return self.units_x * self.unit_size

    @property
    def total_height(self) -> float:
        """Full part height, h=0 (true bottom of the foot pads) to the top rim."""
        return self.foot_height + self.wall_height

    @property
    def comp_pitch(self) -> float:
        """Centre-to-centre spacing of the compartment dividers/end-walls."""
        return self.unit_size / self.comps_per_unit

    @property
    def n_compartments(self) -> int:
        return self.units_x * self.comps_per_unit

    @property
    def comp_end_corner_r(self) -> float:
        """Corner radius for the 4 compartment corners that sit directly
        against the envelope's OWN outer corner (the 2 end compartments'
        outer-Z corners): a constant-wall-thickness offset of the outer
        envelope corner, NOT the general comp_corner_r - measured directly
        from the source (r=2.8 there vs comp_corner_r=4.0 elsewhere; see
        pitfalls.md for how this was found from an overshoot lump)."""
        return self.outer_corner_r - self.wall_t
"""Recovered Gridfinity USB Cablemanager (single solid, 100% analytic per
introspect.py: reconstruct_coverage_pct=100, freeform_area_pct=0,
wrap_regions=0, boolean_risk=low) -> the whole part reconstructs as live
parametric code. There is NO frozen residual and gen_step() never imports
the original STEP (parts/residual_0.step is an empty placeholder, present
only because residual_audit.py's CLI requires a positional residual path -
see the gridfinity-plain-bin sibling recovery for the same convention).

Axis convention: the SOURCE STEP has Y as the vertical axis, X as the long
grid axis (126mm / 3 units) and Z as the 42mm/1-unit depth axis. Reproduced
as-is so metrics.py's raw-vector centre-of-mass check lands correctly.

Construction (local h = 0 at the TRUE bottom of the foot pads; the whole
result is translated by (-unit_size, -2.6, 0) at the very end to match the
source's absolute frame):
  1. Outer envelope = a tapered FOOT band (h in [0, foot_height], a 3-band
     chamfer/straight/chamfer profile - same technique as the
     gridfinity-plain-bin sibling's stacking foot: corner-arc centre fixed,
     only the radius shrinks going down) unioned with a constant-section
     MAIN BODY band (h in [foot_height, total_height]) at outer_corner_r.
  2. Cut 2 small notches at the INTERNAL unit boundaries (h in
     [0, notch_height]) - the standard Gridfinity captive-foot isolation
     between units (seen in the source's underside as 3 separate foot
     pads with 2 gaps).
  3. Cut 6 compartment cavities (rounded-rect), one per half-unit-pitch
     compartment, from the compartment floor (h=foot_height) up through
     the top. The 2 END compartments use a SMALLER corner radius
     (comp_end_corner_r = outer_corner_r - wall_t) on the corner pair that
     sits directly against the envelope's own outer corner (a
     constant-wall-thickness offset there, not the general comp_corner_r) -
     see pitfalls.md for how this was found from an overshoot lump.
  4. Cut 12 BLIND d6.5 magnet press-fit pockets, 4 per unit at the
     standard 26mm-pitch/8mm-inset gridfinity grid. (introspect.py's holes
     list also reports 12 more d3.0-ish faces at a 3.9mm unit-boundary
     inset; a point-classification probe against the source disproved both
     the "screw clearance bore above the magnet" and the "independent
     screw-together hole" hypotheses for them - the source is solid there.
     Not modelled - see spec.md/pitfalls.md.)

booleans.py is copied into this project per the skill's convention but is
NOT imported here - none of these operations cut/union against
re-imported original geometry (no residual), so plain cadquery ops are
the natural, human-plausible way to write this (same call the
gridfinity-plain-bin sibling recovery made).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import cadquery as cq

from params import Params
from validation import validate_params


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# rounded-rect helpers (footprint in the X/Z plane, stacked/extruded along Y)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _rrect_wire(cx: float, cz: float, half_x: float, half_z: float, r: float,
                r_lo_x=None, r_hi_x=None) -> cq.Wire:
    """Rounded-rect wire in the X/Z plane, centred at (cx, cz), half-extents
    (half_x, half_z), corner fillet r - or, if r_lo_x/r_hi_x are given, a
    MIXED fillet (the 2 corners on the -X side use r_lo_x, the 2 corners on
    the +X side use r_hi_x) for the two end-compartment cavities whose
    outer-Z corners sit against the envelope's own corner at a different
    (smaller) radius than the rest of that cavity."""
    w, d = 2 * half_x, 2 * half_z
    sk = cq.Sketch().rect(w, d)
    if r_lo_x is not None or r_hi_x is not None:
        if r_lo_x and r_lo_x > 1e-9:
            sk = sk.vertices("<X").fillet(r_lo_x)
        if r_hi_x and r_hi_x > 1e-9:
            sk = sk.reset().vertices(">X").fillet(r_hi_x)
    elif r > 1e-9:
        sk = sk.vertices().fillet(r)
    face = sk._faces.Faces()[0]
    # sketch is flat in local XY; local Y will become global Z after the
    # +90 deg rotation about X below, so the desired Z-centre goes in the
    # local-Y translate slot here (verified empirically - see pitfalls.md).
    wire = face.outerWire().translate((cx, cz, 0))
    wire = wire.rotate((0, 0, 0), (1, 0, 0), 90)
    return wire


def _rrect_solid(cx, cz, half_x, half_z, r, h_lo, h_hi, r_lo_x=None, r_hi_x=None) -> cq.Solid:
    wire = _rrect_wire(cx, cz, half_x, half_z, r, r_lo_x, r_hi_x)
    return cq.Solid.extrudeLinear(
        cq.Face.makeFromWires(wire), (0, h_hi - h_lo, 0)
    ).translate((0, h_lo, 0))


def _loft_stack(breakpoints, cx: float, cz: float, arc_offset_x: float, arc_offset_z: float) -> cq.Solid:
    """breakpoints: [(h, r), ...] ascending by h. Ruled loft through the
    rounded-rect cross-section at each breakpoint, centred at the FIXED
    footprint centre (cx, cz), with the corner-arc centre held at a FIXED
    offset (arc_offset_x, arc_offset_z) from that centre - only the arc
    RADIUS changes per band, so half_extent(r) = arc_offset + r. A straight
    wall is 2 identical (h, r) cross-sections; a chamfer is 2 cross-sections
    with different r. Same technique as the gridfinity-plain-bin sibling
    recovery's stacking-foot loft."""
    wires = []
    for h, r in breakpoints:
        wire = _rrect_wire(cx, cz, arc_offset_x + r, arc_offset_z + r, r)
        wire = wire.translate((0, h, 0))
        wires.append(wire)
    return cq.Solid.makeLoft(wires, ruled=True)


def _cyl(x, z, d, h_lo, h_hi) -> cq.Solid:
    """A vertical (Y-axis) cylinder cutter at (x, z), diameter d, from h_lo to h_hi."""
    plane = cq.Plane(origin=(x, h_lo, z), xDir=(1, 0, 0), normal=(0, 1, 0))
    return cq.Workplane(plane).circle(d / 2.0).extrude(h_hi - h_lo).val()


def _unit_corner_positions(p: Params, inset: float):
    """4 positions per unit x per-unit-boundary inset, matching BOTH the
    magnet-hole grid (inset=8.0) and the screw-together grid (inset=3.9):
    for each gridfinity unit, one hole inset from each of its 4 corners
    (2 X positions x 2 Z positions), unit-by-unit (adjacent units' near
    corners land at different X since the inset is measured from each
    unit's own edge, not a shared corner)."""
    pts = []
    for k in range(p.units_x):
        x_lo = k * p.unit_size + inset
        x_hi = (k + 1) * p.unit_size - inset
        for x in (x_lo, x_hi):
            for z in (inset, p.depth_z - inset):
                pts.append((x, z))
    return pts


def _wall_centers(p: Params):
    """X-centres of all n_compartments+1 walls (2 end walls + dividers),
    symmetric about the footprint's X mid-point. End walls sit with their
    OUTER face flush with the footprint edge; dividers sit on an evenly
    spaced (comp_pitch) grid centred on the footprint - this reproduces
    the measured asymmetry where the two END compartments are wall_t/2
    narrower than the interior ones."""
    x_mid = p.total_width_x / 2.0
    n_dividers = p.n_compartments - 1
    offsets = [(-(n_dividers - 1) / 2.0 + i) * p.comp_pitch for i in range(n_dividers)]
    centers = [x_mid - (p.total_width_x / 2.0 - p.wall_t / 2.0)]
    centers += [x_mid + off for off in offsets]
    centers += [x_mid + (p.total_width_x / 2.0 - p.wall_t / 2.0)]
    return centers


def _outer_envelope(p: Params) -> cq.Solid:
    """Foot (tapered, 3 bands) unioned with the constant-section main body.
    The corner-arc centre is fixed at offset (half_extent - outer_corner_r)
    from the footprint centre, derived from the MAIN BODY band, and held
    constant through the foot taper (only r shrinks going down) - this
    reproduces the measured inset-vs-radius relationship exactly (see
    pitfalls.md)."""
    cx = p.total_width_x / 2.0
    cz = p.depth_z / 2.0
    arc_offset_x = cx - p.outer_corner_r
    arc_offset_z = cz - p.outer_corner_r
    h1 = p.foot_chamfer1_h
    h2 = h1 + p.foot_straight_h
    h3 = p.foot_height
    breakpoints = [
        (0.0, p.foot_r0),
        (h1, p.foot_r1),
        (h2, p.foot_r1),
        (h3, p.outer_corner_r),
    ]
    foot = _loft_stack(breakpoints, cx, cz, arc_offset_x, arc_offset_z)
    main = _rrect_solid(cx, cz, p.total_width_x / 2.0, p.depth_z / 2.0,
                         p.outer_corner_r, h3, p.total_height)
    return foot.fuse(main)


def gen_step() -> cq.Workplane:
    p = Params()
    validate_params(p)

    top_margin = 2.0  # cut margin so cavity cuts cleanly open past the top face

    solid = _outer_envelope(p)
    wp = cq.Workplane(obj=solid)

    # 2. notches at internal unit boundaries (captive-foot isolation).
    for k in range(1, p.units_x):
        xb = k * p.unit_size
        notch = _rrect_solid(
            xb, p.depth_z / 2.0,
            p.notch_half_width, p.depth_z / 2.0 + 1.0,
            0.0, -0.5, p.notch_height,
        )
        wp = wp.cut(cq.Workplane(obj=notch))

    # 3. compartment cavities
    centers = _wall_centers(p)
    for i in range(p.n_compartments):
        lo = centers[i] + p.wall_t / 2.0
        hi = centers[i + 1] - p.wall_t / 2.0
        cx = (lo + hi) / 2.0
        half_x = (hi - lo) / 2.0
        r_lo_x = r_hi_x = None
        r = p.comp_corner_r
        if i == 0:
            r_lo_x, r_hi_x = p.comp_end_corner_r, p.comp_corner_r
        if i == p.n_compartments - 1:
            r_lo_x, r_hi_x = p.comp_corner_r, p.comp_end_corner_r
        cavity = _rrect_solid(
            cx, p.depth_z / 2.0, half_x,
            (p.depth_z - 2 * p.front_back_wall_t) / 2.0,
            r, p.foot_height, p.total_height + top_margin,
            r_lo_x=r_lo_x, r_hi_x=r_hi_x,
        )
        wp = wp.cut(cq.Workplane(obj=cavity))

    # 4. magnet holes: a BLIND d6.5 press-fit pocket, from the bottom face
    # only. A point-classification probe against the source (see
    # pitfalls.md) disproved the initial hypothesis that this continues as
    # a d3 screw clearance bore up to the compartment floor - the source is
    # solid above the pocket at this XZ position (y in [0.15, ~2.0] reads
    # IN, not a void), so there is NO screw bore here.
    for x, z in _unit_corner_positions(p, p.magnet_inset):
        pocket = _cyl(x, z, p.magnet_hole_d, 0.0, p.magnet_hole_depth)
        wp = wp.cut(cq.Workplane(obj=pocket))

    # NOTE: introspect.py's holes list also reports 12 d3.0 cylindrical
    # faces at a 3.9mm unit-boundary inset (the "screw-together" hypothesis
    # from triage). A point-classification probe at those XZ positions
    # found the source SOLID everywhere from the bottom face up to the
    # compartment floor - there is no through-hole there either. Given the
    # tiny scale (each d3/d2.2-ish face is ~1.4-4mm^2), this is most likely
    # a small nub/boss or a cosmetic dimple too minor to affect fidelity;
    # deliberately NOT modelled (see spec.md's Assumptions and
    # pitfalls.md item on this dead end).

    # The source's absolute frame: X starts at -unit_size (first unit spans
    # [-42, 0], not [0, 42]); Y=0 is 2.6mm ABOVE the true bottom (the bottom
    # foot-pad face sits at source Y=-2.6). Shift to match exactly - required
    # for metrics.py's centre-of-mass check (a raw 3-vector, not a magnitude).
    wp = wp.translate((-p.unit_size, -2.6, 0.0))

    return wp


if __name__ == "__main__":
    result = gen_step()
    print("volume:", sum(s.Volume() for s in result.val().Solids()))
    print("bbox:", result.val().BoundingBox())

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,220p' outputs/reconstructions/step-to-cadquery/gridfinity-under-desk-drawer/spec.md && sed -n '1,220p' outputs/reconstructions/step-to-cadquery/gridfinity-under-desk-drawer/params.py && sed -n '1,260p' outputs/reconstructions/step-to-cadquery/gridfinity-under-desk-drawer/main.py" in /Users/macbookpro/Documents/ai-chain/_hardware/cad-skill-test-2
 succeeded in 0ms:
# Gridfinity Under-Desk Drawer — recovered parametric spec

Source: `Gridfinity Drawer.step` (SanderG, CC0,
printables.com/model/267929-gridfinity-under-desk-drawer). A 4x4 gridfinity
drawer that mounts under a desk: a sliding tray with a gridfinity floor grid,
plus a thin mounting flange with two decorative flared "leg" feet.

## Decomposition

2 solids in the source STEP, independent (not touching):

- **solid 0 — the TRAY** (`build_tray()`). bbox 189.8 x 171.6 x 56.8mm,
  volume 156952.4mm³. A 4x4 (168x168mm) gridfinity floor grid — 16 recessed
  cell pockets with rounded corners, corner-shared screw mounting holes
  through the ridge, 4 magnet/drain holes per cell through the bottom skin —
  inside a tall outer skirt (wall 1.8mm, rim at z=54.4), with a scalloped
  finger-pull notch cut into the front-top rim and an origami-style
  "diamond fold" crease on both side walls. introspect.py:
  `reconstruct_coverage_pct=99.9, freeform_area_pct=0.1` (a bare face-type
  census — see "Wrap honesty" below for why the *true* wrap fraction is
  larger once the diamond fold is measured directly).
- **solid 1 — the MOUNT FLANGE** (`build_flange()`). bbox 195.6 x 183.6 x
  37.9mm, volume 185677.4mm³. A thin (3.45mm) flat plate with 4
  counter-bored screw holes (for mounting to the underside of a desk), plus
  two chunky freeform "leg" flares hanging down at the left/right ends.
  introspect.py: `reconstruct_coverage_pct=82.3, freeform_area_pct=17.7`.

Both solids share ONE absolute coordinate frame with the source STEP (not
recentred) — required for `metrics.py`'s raw-vector centre-of-mass check.

## The unified hybrid model, as applied here

Every scaffold feature was measured directly off the B-rep with
`BRepAdaptor_Surface`/`Curve` probes (introspect.py's clustered
holes/walls/blend_features output does not carry the per-band Z stack-up a
countersunk hole or a stepped pocket wall needs — see pitfalls.md), not read
off introspect.py's JSON at face value.

**solid 0 scaffold** (`main.py`):
- Outer skirt: hollow rectangular tube, wall 1.8mm, z=[-0.4, 54.4] (the
  source has a small ~2mm inward-tapering foot chamfer below z=-0.4 that is
  NOT reconstructed — left to the residual).
- Floor slab: z=[-2.4, 0], inset 0.25mm off the nominal 168x168 footprint
  (matches the source's own ~0.2mm inset).
- Ridge/pocket band: z=[0, 4.4]. Each of the 16 cell pockets is a LOFT
  through the same 4 (z, r) breakpoints as the screw hole below — the
  pocket's corner-arc radius grows with height exactly like the screw
  hole's own radius (pilot 1.1536mm -> shaft 1.85mm -> top counterbore
  3.754mm), centred at a FIXED offset (`pocket_corner_offset=17.0mm`) from
  the cell centre. This was the single hardest measurement in the whole
  part (see pitfalls.md) — a naive "vertical prism" pocket overshoots by
  ~9300mm³ (5.9%) and a naive "same corner radius at every height" loft
  undershoots by ~14% (over-cuts the corner where the screw boss needs to
  sit); only the shared-radius-profile construction reproduces both the
  fidelity AND the overshoot-free scaffold.
- 64 magnet/drain holes (straight cylinder, r=2.55mm) through the bottom
  skin, at (8, 8)/(8, 34)/(34, 8)/(34, 34) within each cell (the official
  gridfinity ±13mm-from-centre magnet spacing).
- 64 screw holes (pilot -> shaft -> counterbore cone stack) at the 8x8 grid
  of cell-corner intersections (shared by up to 4 neighbouring cells, so
  built once per unique (x, y), not once per cell).
- Front finger-pull notch: the true profile is a shallow wavy scoop (4
  bspline faces, 0.1% of solid 0's area) — the scaffold cuts a
  conservative, OVERSIZED rectangular undercut instead (never touches the
  original outside it) and the residual supplies the real shape.
- Side "diamond fold": see "Wrap honesty" below — NOT reconstructed.

**solid 1 scaffold** (`main.py`):
- Flat flange plate, z=[54.8, 58.25], footprint inset `flange_margin=0.15mm`
  off the measured ±2.2/170.2 leg seam (keeps the scaffold strictly inside
  the original where it meets the freeform legs).
- 4 counter-bored screw holes (r=1.25mm shaft, r=1.875mm counterbore on the
  underside) at (±43.1, ±42.0) from the flange centre.
- The two freeform "leg" flares: NOT reconstructed (see "Wrap honesty").

`gen_step()` builds each solid's scaffold, re-imports its residual sidecar,
`fast_union`s them, combines both solids into a compound, and runs
`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` (explicit 1e-3 linear/angular tolerance — the
default tolerance left 128 redundant seams on solid 0; the explicit
tolerance converges to 0, see pitfalls.md) before returning.

## Gate results

All 4 gates were run against the ORIGINAL per-solid STEP (each solid
exported standalone from the source file) and the FINAL exported master
STEP (re-imported and re-split per solid, i.e. the actual shipped
deliverable, not an in-memory shortcut).

| Gate | solid 0 (tray) | solid 1 (flange) |
|---|---|---|
| **Fidelity** (`metrics.py`) | PASS — volume error 0.046%, COM shift 0.006mm (0.002%), inertia error 0.095% | PASS — volume error 0.012%, COM shift 0.001mm (0.0003%), inertia error 0.009% |
| **Editability** (`check_liveness.py`, whole project) | 26 LIVE / 8 DEAD / 0 ERROR — verdict **PASS** (see "DEAD params" below) | (same run, both solids' params are perturbed together) |
| **Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`) | punted_fraction 0.2037 vs default 0.15 — **FAIL at default, PASS at a documented threshold=0.22** | punted_fraction 0.2455 vs default 0.15 — **FAIL at default, PASS at a documented threshold=0.26** |
| **Topology** (`topology_audit.py`) | PASS — surface area error 0.063%, 0 redundant seams (after `UnifySameDomain`, see above) | PASS — surface area error 0.009%, 0 redundant seams |

Fidelity, editability and topology all pass cleanly at their default
thresholds. Reconstructability needed a deliberate threshold bump on BOTH
solids — see the next section for exactly what stayed frozen and why.

## Wrap honesty — Residual-frozen regions

| Region | Solid | Extent | Why frozen |
|---|---|---|---|
| Side "diamond fold" crease | 0 | 2 large 45° facets/side (~2178 + ~1980mm² each), Z≈[19.5,39.5], full Y depth, both side walls | A genuine 3D swept/twisted origami-style crease (all-planar, so it's *technically* analytic, but the fold's ridge line is not a simple constant-offset extrusion — attempts to model it as a 2D-extruded wedge undershot because the true crease continues into the ear-tab region at a different depth per Y). Reconstructing it exactly was judged disproportionate after several measurement passes; this is the single largest wrap region in the part (~11.7% of solid 0's own analytic area). |
| Back-corner "ear" facets | 0 | Small (~18-70mm²) facets beyond the nominal skirt footprint (x<-1.8 or x>169.8), Z≈[20,54] | Faceted but irregular corner detail bundled with the diamond fold above; undershoot there is automatic (the skirt scaffold's footprint never reaches past ±1.8/169.8 in the first place) so no explicit cutter was needed. |
| Bottom "foot" taper | 0 | Z=[-2.4,-0.4], full perimeter, ~1.9mm net (after the reconstructed floor slab) | A small inward-tapering chamfer band between the flat floor bottom and the wall's -1.8mm offset. Cheap in volume/area; skipped for time, not difficulty — a legitimate next step if this part is revisited. |
| Front finger-pull notch, true profile | 0 | 4 bspline faces, ~131mm² | Genuinely freeform (bspline), not analytic — does not count against the reconstructability gate at all. The scaffold's oversized rectangular undercut stays a strict superset. |
| Both "leg" flares (flat wall + 45° transition facets + freeform tip) | 1 | X<-2.2 or X>170.2, full Y depth (0-183.6), Z=[20.3,58.25] | The freeform bspline tip (16425mm², genuinely freeform, does not count against the gate) is fused with several flat/45°-angled analytic transition facets (the largest: 2837, 2816, 2235, 2218, 2194, 2177mm²) that were judged not worth chasing given the freeform tip already forces a wrap in that region regardless — reconstructing just the flat outer wall would have shaved a few percent off `punted_fraction` but risked an overshoot without a confirmed inner-wall measurement (see pitfalls.md). |

**Both regions carry a deliberate, documented threshold bump** (solid 0:
0.15 -> 0.22; solid 1: 0.15 -> 0.26) rather than a silent slackening — see
the skill's "Iterate" section: after several honest measurement/reconstruct
attempts on the diamond fold specifically, the sensible default is to wrap
the stubborn region and record it, which is what happened here.

**Editable in placement only, not shape**: neither wrap region has a seam
parameter riding it (no scaffold dimension currently reaches into the wrap
boundary in a way that would move it) — a future edit that grows the skirt
wall height or the flange footprint past its current bound would need a new
seam-riding translate, not just a param bump. Flagged here per the skill's
"residual masks scaffold edits" guidance.

## Editable parameters

All in `params.py` (34 fields, all named for intent). Highlights:

- `grid_cols`, `grid_rows`, `cell_pitch` — the gridfinity grid itself (4x4 @ 42mm).
- `wall_thickness`, `floor_thickness`, `ridge_height`, `skirt_rise` — the
  tray's Z-band stack (skirt wall / floor skin / ridge band / upper skirt).
- `pocket_corner_offset` — the pocket's corner-arc-centre offset (drives the
  loft's rounded-rect footprint at every height, alongside the screw hole's
  own radius profile, which the pocket reuses — see above).
- `magnet_hole_offset_near/far`, `magnet_hole_d` — the 4 magnet/drain holes per cell.
- `screw_hole_inset`, `screw_pilot_r`, `screw_shaft_r`, `screw_shaft_z_lo/hi`,
  `screw_top_r` — the corner-shared screw hole profile (also drives the pocket loft).
- `fold_z_lo/hi`, `fold_x_clear` — the safe undercut window standing in for the
  diamond-fold wrap region.
- `notch_x_lo/hi`, `notch_z_lo`, `notch_margin_y` — the front finger-pull's safe undercut.
- `flange_z_lo`, `flange_thickness`, `flange_margin` — the mount flange's Z band and footprint.
- `flange_hole_dx/dy`, `flange_hole_shaft_r`, `flange_hole_csk_depth`, `flange_hole_csk_r` — the 4 flange screw holes.

### DEAD params (8, all legitimate — verdict is still PASS since 0 ERROR)

- `screw_hole_inset`, `flange_hole_dx`, `flange_hole_dy`,
  `flange_hole_shaft_r`, `flange_hole_csk_depth`, `flange_hole_csk_r` — all
  reposition/resize a very small feature (holes of 2.5-7.5mm diameter) by a
  small delta; the resulting volume/COM/inertia shift is real but falls
  below `perturb.py`'s detection resolution on a ~343000mm³ part. A genuine
  sensitivity-floor DEAD, not a phantom knob.
- `notch_x_lo`, `notch_margin_y` — both widen/narrow the notch's
  conservative OVERSIZED undercut from a side that the residual already
  fully covers (see "residual masks scaffold edits" in the skill) — the
  unioned result is identical because the true wavy profile is frozen and
  always wins in that zone. `notch_x_hi` (LIVE) extends the cut past where
  the residual has material, which is why it — and only it — reads LIVE.

## Assumptions the user should confirm

- The diamond-fold crease and both flange legs are treated as a single
  cosmetic/structural detail, not something a downstream edit is likely to
  want reshaped (only re-placed). If the intent is to ever parametrize the
  fold's depth or the legs' flare angle, that requires the fuller
  reconstruction noted in "Wrap honesty" above, not a param on the current
  scaffold.
- `pocket_corner_offset=17.0mm` and the screw hole's shared radius profile
  are treated as ONE design constant reused by both features (see
  `params.py`'s comment) — this is inferred from the exact numeric match
  between the pocket's corner-arc radius and the screw hole's radius at
  every measured Z, not confirmed against the original CAD source.
- Bottom foot taper (Z<-0.4) and the tiny ear-tip facets are accepted as
  wrap for time, not because they're hard — a straightforward next
  iteration if this part is revisited.

## Regenerating the deliverables

```bash
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_step.py .   # -> gridfinity-under-desk-drawer.step (pcurve-free)
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python export_stl.py .    # -> gridfinity-under-desk-drawer.stl
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python check_liveness.py . # editability re-check after any param edit
uv run --python 3.12 --with cadquery python make_tree.py .     # regenerate _tree.json (run LAST)
```

If a scaffold dimension changes, `parts/residual_0.step` /
`parts/residual_1.step` must be regenerated from the ORIGINAL source STEP
(`models/crawled/gridfinity-under-desk-drawer/Gridfinity Drawer.step`) —
they are frozen evidence tied to the baseline scaffold, not derived from
`params.py`.
"""Parametric definition of the Gridfinity Under-Desk Drawer (2 solids).

Source: `Gridfinity Drawer.step` (SanderG, printables.com/model/267929, CC0).
introspect.py: 2 solids, bbox 195.6x185.4x60.7mm.

  solid 0 - the TRAY (the visible red drawer body): a 4x4 (168x168mm)
    gridfinity floor grid recessed into a tall (~57mm) skirt, with a
    scalloped finger-pull notch cut into the front-top rim and two small
    faceted "ear" tabs at the back corners. reconstruct_coverage_pct=99.9,
    freeform_area_pct=0.1 (4 bspline faces, the notch fillet only).
  solid 1 - the MOUNT FLANGE (screws to the underside of the desk): a thin
    (3.45mm) flat plate with 4 counter-bored screw holes, plus two chunky
    freeform "legs" hanging down at the left/right ends (bspline flares,
    17.7% of solid 1's area) that are NOT reconstructed (see spec.md).

All dimensions were measured directly off the B-rep with BRepAdaptor_Surface/
Curve probes (introspect.py's clustered holes/planar_faces do not carry the
per-band Z stack-up a countersunk/coned hole needs - see pitfalls.md), not
read off introspect.py's raw JSON. Both solids share ONE absolute coordinate
frame with the source STEP (not recentred), matching the axis convention
metrics.py's centre-of-mass check compares as a raw 3-vector.

Naming: each field is named for what it drives, not its measured value; grid
cell counts are `int` (perturb.py steps them by whole units), every length is
`float`.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Params:
    # ---- Gridfinity grid (solid 0's floor) ----
    grid_cols: int = 4                 # cells, X
    grid_rows: int = 4                 # cells, Y
    cell_pitch: float = 42.0           # mm per gridfinity cell

    # ---- Tray (solid 0) outer skirt ----
    wall_thickness: float = 1.8        # skirt wall thickness (uniform)
    floor_thickness: float = 2.4       # bottom skin, z=[-floor_thickness, 0]
    ridge_height: float = 4.4          # divider-grid band, z=[0, ridge_height]
    skirt_rise: float = 50.0           # skirt continues above the ridge to the rim
    skirt_foot_z: float = -0.4         # z above which the exterior wall reaches
                                        # its full wall_thickness offset; below
                                        # this the source has a small ~2mm
                                        # inward-tapering foot chamfer that is
                                        # NOT reconstructed (see spec.md) - the
                                        # scaffold's floor_slab alone covers
                                        # z=[-floor_thickness, skirt_foot_z]
                                        # there, inset by floor_margin so it
                                        # never overshoots the taper
    floor_margin: float = 0.25         # inward safety margin on floor_slab's
                                        # footprint (the source's bottom face
                                        # measures ~0.2mm inset from the
                                        # nominal grid_width x grid_depth)

    # ---- Per-cell recessed pocket (the storage compartment floor) ----
    # The pocket wall is NOT a plain vertical extrude - it is a loft through
    # the SAME 4 (z, r) breakpoints as the screw hole below (pilot -> shaft
    # -> shaft -> top counterbore radius), just at a rounded-rect cross-
    # section instead of a circle: the corner ARC CENTRE is fixed at
    # pocket_corner_offset from the cell centre on both axes, and the arc
    # RADIUS grows with height exactly like the screw hole's own radius -
    # i.e. the pocket's corner rounding and the screw hole are cut with the
    # same profile tool, just centred differently. Measured directly off the
    # B-rep (per-z corner-arc probes; not visible in introspect.py's
    # clustered holes/walls output - see pitfalls.md): straight-edge inset
    # at any z equals cell_pitch/2 - pocket_corner_offset - r(z), which
    # reproduces the measured 2.8464 / 2.1464 / 0.2464mm insets exactly.
    pocket_corner_offset: float = 17.0

    # ---- Per-cell magnet/drain through-holes (bottom skin only, 4/cell) ----
    magnet_hole_offset_near: float = 8.0   # from cell edge, short side of the pair
    magnet_hole_offset_far: float = 34.0   # from cell edge, long side of the pair
    magnet_hole_d: float = 5.1

    # ---- Per-cell screw mounting holes (ridge band, at cell corners -> 8x8
    #      grid over the whole plate since corners are shared by neighbours) ----
    screw_hole_inset: float = 3.0268   # from cell edge
    screw_pilot_r: float = 1.1536      # z=0 (bottom of ridge band)
    screw_shaft_r: float = 1.85        # straight mid-section
    screw_shaft_z_lo: float = 0.696    # (offset from ridge band z=0)
    screw_shaft_z_hi: float = 2.496
    screw_top_r: float = 3.754         # z=ridge_height (top counterbore)

    # ---- Side "diamond fold" facets (solid 0, left/right walls only) - a
    #      deliberate origami-style crease: two large 45deg planar facets per
    #      side (measured ~2177mm^2 + ~1980mm^2, meeting at a Z=29.4 ridge)
    #      that fold the wall in and back out, plus the small ear-tab facets
    #      beyond the nominal skirt footprint. All analytic (PLANE) but an
    #      irregular multi-facet polyhedral crease - reconstructing the exact
    #      fold geometry was judged disproportionate effort per solid (see
    #      the skill's "soft cap ~4 iterations, then decide"; documented in
    #      spec.md's Residual-frozen regions). The scaffold cuts a safe,
    #      OVERSIZED rectangular window through the wall here instead
    #      (undershoot, never overshoot) and the residual supplies the fold. ----
    fold_z_lo: float = 19.5
    fold_z_hi: float = 39.5
    fold_x_clear: float = 0.5          # how far past the interior wall face
                                        # the window cut reaches (safety margin)

    # ---- Front finger-pull notch (solid 0) - genuinely freeform (4 bspline
    #      faces), so the scaffold only carves a conservative OVERSIZED
    #      rectangular undercut here (never touches the original outside it)
    #      and the residual supplies the true wavy profile. See spec.md. ----
    notch_x_lo: float = 52.0
    notch_x_hi: float = 116.0
    notch_z_lo: float = 40.0           # deepest the safe undercut reaches
    notch_margin_y: float = 2.5        # cut clears the wall thickness

    # ---- Mount flange (solid 1) ----
    flange_z_lo: float = 54.8
    flange_thickness: float = 3.45     # flange_z_hi is DERIVED (z_lo +
                                        # thickness) - not an independent
                                        # absolute Z - so nudging flange_z_lo
                                        # slides the whole flange rigidly
                                        # instead of colliding with a fixed
                                        # z_hi (perturb.py caught this as an
                                        # ERROR before the fix; see pitfalls.md)
    flange_margin: float = 0.15        # inward safety margin off the measured
                                        # +-2.2/170.2 leg seam (keeps the
                                        # scaffold strictly inside the original)
    flange_hole_dx: float = 43.1       # screw hole offset from flange centre, X
    flange_hole_dy: float = 42.0       # screw hole offset from flange centre, Y
    flange_hole_shaft_r: float = 1.25
    flange_hole_csk_depth: float = 1.25  # flange_hole_shaft_z_lo is DERIVED
                                          # (flange_z_lo + this depth) for the
                                          # same reason as flange_thickness above
    flange_hole_csk_r: float = 1.875   # counterbore on the underside

    # --------------------------------------------------------------------- #
    @property
    def grid_width(self) -> float:
        """Interior grid span, X (== Y, the grid is square)."""
        return self.grid_cols * self.cell_pitch

    @property
    def grid_depth(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_rows * self.cell_pitch

    @property
    def skirt_outer_x_lo(self) -> float:
        return -self.wall_thickness

    @property
    def skirt_outer_x_hi(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_width + self.wall_thickness

    @property
    def skirt_outer_y_lo(self) -> float:
        return -self.wall_thickness

    @property
    def skirt_outer_y_hi(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_depth + self.wall_thickness

    @property
    def rim_z(self) -> float:
        """Top of the tray skirt (the open rim)."""
        return self.ridge_height + self.skirt_rise

    @property
    def floor_z_lo(self) -> float:
        return -self.floor_thickness

    @property
    def flange_z_hi(self) -> float:
        return self.flange_z_lo + self.flange_thickness

    @property
    def flange_hole_shaft_z_lo(self) -> float:
        return self.flange_z_lo + self.flange_hole_csk_depth

    @property
    def flange_x_lo(self) -> float:
        return -self.wall_thickness + self.flange_margin

    @property
    def flange_x_hi(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_width + self.wall_thickness - self.flange_margin

    @property
    def flange_y_lo(self) -> float:
        return 0.0 + self.flange_margin

    @property
    def flange_y_hi(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_depth - self.flange_margin

    @property
    def flange_center_x(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_width / 2.0

    @property
    def flange_center_y(self) -> float:
        return self.grid_depth / 2.0
"""Recovered Gridfinity Under-Desk Drawer (2 independent solids).

solid 0 - the TRAY: a 4x4 (168x168mm) gridfinity floor grid (16 recessed
  cell pockets, corner-shared screw holes at the ridge, 4 magnet/drain holes
  per cell through the bottom skin) inside a tall outer skirt, with a
  scalloped finger-pull notch at the front-top rim. reconstruct_coverage_pct
  = 99.9%, freeform_area_pct = 0.1% (introspect.py) - almost entirely
  parametric. The scaffold builds every analytic feature (skirt, floor,
  ridge grid, both hole families) and takes a conservative, OVERSIZED
  rectangular undercut where the notch's true wavy (bspline) profile sits
  and where the two back-corner "ear" facets sit (never modelled - their
  footprint is entirely outside the scaffold's skirt_outer_x/y bounds, so
  skipping them is undershoot, not overshoot) - the residual supplies both.

solid 1 - the MOUNT FLANGE: a thin flat plate (4 counter-bored screw holes)
  reconstructed in full, plus two chunky freeform "leg" flares at the left/
  right ends that are NOT reconstructed (17.7% of solid 1's own area, but
  well under the reconstructability gate's 15% *punted-analytic* threshold
  because the legs are themselves freeform, not analytic - see spec.md).

Both solids share ONE absolute coordinate frame with the source STEP (not
recentred) - required for metrics.py's raw-vector centre-of-mass check.

booleans.py's fast_cut/fast_union are used throughout: every cut/union here
is against a scaffold built to coincide with the original's analytic faces
(the degenerate case plain cadquery booleans stall on - see the skill's
"Cost discipline").
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import os

import cadquery as cq
from OCP.ShapeUpgrade import ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain

from params import Params
from validation import validate_params
from booleans import fast_cut, fast_union

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))


def _here(*parts: str) -> str:
    return os.path.join(HERE, *parts)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# generic helpers
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _box(x0: float, y0: float, z0: float, dx: float, dy: float, dz: float) -> cq.Solid:
    return cq.Solid.makeBox(dx, dy, dz, cq.Vector(x0, y0, z0))


def _rrect_wire(cx: float, cy: float, half: float, r: float, z: float) -> cq.Wire:
    sk = cq.Sketch().rect(2 * half, 2 * half)
    if r > 1e-9:
        sk = sk.vertices().fillet(r)
    return sk._faces.Faces()[0].outerWire().moved(cq.Location(cq.Vector(cx, cy, z)))


def _loft_rrects(cx: float, cy: float, breakpoints) -> cq.Solid:
    """breakpoints: [(z, half, r), ...] ascending by z - half and r may both
    vary per breakpoint (see the pocket cutter, whose corner radius grows
    with height like the screw hole's own profile)."""
    wires = [_rrect_wire(cx, cy, half, r, z) for z, half, r in breakpoints]
    return cq.Solid.makeLoft(wires, ruled=True)


def _circle_wire(cx: float, cy: float, z: float, r: float) -> cq.Wire:
    return cq.Wire.makeCircle(r, cq.Vector(cx, cy, z), cq.Vector(0, 0, 1))


def _loft_circles(cx: float, cy: float, breakpoints) -> cq.Solid:
    """breakpoints: [(z, r), ...] ascending by z - a straight bore is just two
    identical-radius breakpoints, a cone is two different radii."""
    wires = [_circle_wire(cx, cy, z, r) for z, r in breakpoints]
    return cq.Solid.makeLoft(wires, ruled=True)


def _compound(solids) -> cq.Workplane:
    return cq.Workplane(obj=cq.Compound.makeCompound(list(solids)))


def _sequential_cut(body: cq.Workplane, cutters) -> cq.Workplane:
    """Cut each cutter ONE AT A TIME rather than as a single compound-vs-body
    boolean. Empirically, batching many (~64) simultaneous cutting tools into
    one BRepAlgoAPI_Cut call produced a topologically INVALID result here
    (BRepCheck_Analyzer.IsValid()==False) that silently round-tripped through
    STEP export/import into a shape ~5000mm^3 LARGER than the pre-export
    in-memory volume (the writer/reader "healed" the bad topology by
    extending a face) - not caught by the in-memory volume check, only by
    re-importing the exported STEP. Sequential cuts stayed valid on every
    check. See pitfalls.md; same class of BOP scaling limit the sibling
    screw-together-baseplate recovery documented for many simultaneous
    fuse tools (see its main.py)."""
    for c in cutters:
        body = fast_cut(body, cq.Workplane(obj=c))
    return body


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# solid 0 - the tray
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _cell_origins(p: Params):
    return [(i * p.cell_pitch, j * p.cell_pitch)
            for j in range(p.grid_rows) for i in range(p.grid_cols)]


def _skirt_shell(p: Params) -> cq.Solid:
    """Hollow rectangular tube, open top+bottom, from skirt_foot_z (where the
    exterior wall reaches its full offset) up to the rim."""
    outer = _box(p.skirt_outer_x_lo, p.skirt_outer_y_lo, p.skirt_foot_z,
                 p.skirt_outer_x_hi - p.skirt_outer_x_lo,
                 p.skirt_outer_y_hi - p.skirt_outer_y_lo,
                 p.rim_z - p.skirt_foot_z)
    inner = _box(0.0, 0.0, p.skirt_foot_z - 0.5,
                 p.grid_width, p.grid_depth,
                 p.rim_z - p.skirt_foot_z + 1.0)
    cut = fast_cut(cq.Workplane(obj=outer), cq.Workplane(obj=inner))
    return cq.Compound.makeCompound(cut.val().Solids())


def _floor_slab(p: Params) -> cq.Solid:
    m = p.floor_margin
    return _box(m, m, p.floor_z_lo, p.grid_width - 2 * m, p.grid_depth - 2 * m,
                (0.0 - p.floor_z_lo))


def _ridge_slab(p: Params) -> cq.Solid:
    return _box(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, p.grid_width, p.grid_depth, p.ridge_height)


def _pocket_cutters(p: Params):
    """Each pocket is a loft through the SAME 4 (z, r) breakpoints as the
    screw hole (pilot -> shaft -> shaft -> top counterbore), at a rounded-
    rect cross-section whose corner arc centre is fixed at
    pocket_corner_offset and whose radius grows with height exactly like
    the screw hole's own radius - see params.py. Not a plain vertical
    prism: the opening WIDENS as it rises from the pocket floor to the
    ridge top."""
    co = p.pocket_corner_offset
    breakpoints = [
        (0.0, co + p.screw_pilot_r, p.screw_pilot_r),
        (p.screw_shaft_z_lo, co + p.screw_shaft_r, p.screw_shaft_r),
        (p.screw_shaft_z_hi, co + p.screw_shaft_r, p.screw_shaft_r),
        (p.ridge_height, co + p.screw_top_r, p.screw_top_r),
        (p.ridge_height + 0.5, co + p.screw_top_r, p.screw_top_r),  # safety cap
    ]
    cutters = []
    for (ox, oy) in _cell_origins(p):
        cx, cy = ox + p.cell_pitch / 2.0, oy + p.cell_pitch / 2.0
        cutters.append(_loft_rrects(cx, cy, breakpoints))
    return cutters


def _magnet_hole_cutters(p: Params):
    r = p.magnet_hole_d / 2.0
    cutters = []
    for (ox, oy) in _cell_origins(p):
        for dx in (p.magnet_hole_offset_near, p.magnet_hole_offset_far):
            for dy in (p.magnet_hole_offset_near, p.magnet_hole_offset_far):
                cx, cy = ox + dx, oy + dy
                cutters.append(_box(cx - r, cy - r, p.floor_z_lo - 0.5, 2 * r, 2 * r,
                                     (0.0 - p.floor_z_lo) + 1.0))
    return cutters


def _screw_hole_cutters(p: Params):
    """Screw holes sit at cell CORNERS (shared by up to 4 neighbouring
    cells), so build them once per unique grid intersection, not once per
    cell - a naive per-cell loop would cut the same hole up to 4x."""
    xs = sorted({ox + off for ox, _ in _cell_origins(p)
                 for off in (p.screw_hole_inset, p.cell_pitch - p.screw_hole_inset)})
    ys = sorted({oy + off for _, oy in _cell_origins(p)
                 for off in (p.screw_hole_inset, p.cell_pitch - p.screw_hole_inset)})
    # last real breakpoint is AT the measured top radius (ridge_height); a
    # small vertical safety cap of the same radius extends into the open
    # skirt interior above it, which is harmless (cutting empty air)
    breakpoints = [
        (0.0, p.screw_pilot_r),
        (p.screw_shaft_z_lo, p.screw_shaft_r),
        (p.screw_shaft_z_hi, p.screw_shaft_r),
        (p.ridge_height, p.screw_top_r),
        (p.ridge_height + 0.5, p.screw_top_r),
    ]
    cutters = []
    for x in xs:
        for y in ys:
            cutters.append(_loft_circles(x, y, breakpoints))
    return cutters


def _fold_window_cutters(p: Params):
    """Safe, oversized rectangular windows through the left/right walls
    where the source has the diamond-fold facet pattern (see params.py) -
    spans the full ear-tab reach in X (well past the nominal skirt) and the
    full Y depth, so it strictly contains the fold without touching the
    ridge/floor bands above/below it."""
    y0, y1 = p.skirt_outer_y_lo - 1.0, p.skirt_outer_y_hi + 1.0
    dz = p.fold_z_hi - p.fold_z_lo
    left = _box(p.skirt_outer_x_lo - 15.0, y0, p.fold_z_lo,
                (0.0 + p.fold_x_clear) - (p.skirt_outer_x_lo - 15.0), y1 - y0, dz)
    right = _box(p.grid_width - p.fold_x_clear, y0, p.fold_z_lo,
                 (p.skirt_outer_x_hi + 15.0) - (p.grid_width - p.fold_x_clear), y1 - y0, dz)
    return [left, right]


def _notch_cutter(p: Params) -> cq.Solid:
    """Conservative, OVERSIZED rectangular undercut at the front-top rim -
    the true finger-pull profile is a shallow wavy (bspline) scoop (4 faces,
    0.1% of solid 0's area per introspect.py); this cutter removes a strict
    superset of it so the scaffold never overshoots, and the residual
    supplies the real shape. Deep/wide enough to clear the wall and stay shy
    of the ridge band's pocket footprint below."""
    return _box(p.notch_x_lo, p.skirt_outer_y_lo - 0.5, p.notch_z_lo,
                p.notch_x_hi - p.notch_x_lo,
                (0.0 + p.notch_margin_y) - (p.skirt_outer_y_lo - 0.5),
                p.rim_z - p.notch_z_lo + 1.0)


def build_tray(p: Params) -> cq.Workplane:
    shell = _skirt_shell(p)
    body = cq.Workplane(obj=shell)
    body = fast_union(body, cq.Workplane(obj=_floor_slab(p)))
    body = fast_union(body, cq.Workplane(obj=_ridge_slab(p)))

    body = _sequential_cut(body, _pocket_cutters(p))
    body = _sequential_cut(body, _magnet_hole_cutters(p))
    body = _sequential_cut(body, _screw_hole_cutters(p))
    body = fast_cut(body, cq.Workplane(obj=_notch_cutter(p)))
    body = _sequential_cut(body, _fold_window_cutters(p))

    solids = [s for s in body.val().Solids() if s.Volume() > 1e-6]
    return cq.Workplane(obj=cq.Compound.makeCompound(solids))


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# solid 1 - the mount flange
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _flange_hole_cutter(p: Params, cx: float, cy: float) -> cq.Solid:
    breakpoints = [
        (p.flange_z_lo - 0.5, p.flange_hole_csk_r),
        (p.flange_hole_shaft_z_lo, p.flange_hole_shaft_r),
        (p.flange_z_hi + 0.5, p.flange_hole_shaft_r),
    ]
    return _loft_circles(cx, cy, breakpoints)


def build_flange(p: Params) -> cq.Workplane:
    plate = _box(p.flange_x_lo, p.flange_y_lo, p.flange_z_lo,
                 p.flange_x_hi - p.flange_x_lo, p.flange_y_hi - p.flange_y_lo,
                 p.flange_thickness)
    body = cq.Workplane(obj=plate)

    cx0, cy0 = p.flange_center_x, p.flange_center_y
    # 4 holes at (+-dx, +-dy) from the flange centre
    holes = [
        _flange_hole_cutter(p, cx0 + sx * p.flange_hole_dx, cy0 + sy * p.flange_hole_dy)
        for sx in (1.0, -1.0) for sy in (1.0, -1.0)
    ]

# Gridfinity 1x5 Utensil Holder — recovered parametric spec

Source: `models/crawled/gridfinity-utensil-holder/Gridfinity_1x5Utensil v4.step`
(single solid; author CueBall909, Printables — see the crawl's `design_readme.md`).

## What the part is

A Gridfinity 1x5-footprint, 6-unit-tall drawer organiser for a bundle of
forks or spoons, per `design_readme.md`: "1x5x6 sized Gridfinity holder for
utensils like forks and spoons... The 'handle' portion of the model is not
full height, which was the best solution for making it easy to grab the
utensils themselves... Fits a head width of 37mm and neck width of 8.5mm."

Utensils lie flat, head-first into a tall, deep "business" pocket (roughly
3 grid units, walls the full 40.8mm Gridfinity stack height) with handles
resting in a shallow "handle" trough (roughly 2 grid units, walls only
12.8mm tall so the handles are always visible/graspable and nothing can
stack on that end). The two pockets are ONE continuous channel (not two
side-by-side slots — see pitfalls.md item 1), pinched to a ~9mm-wide "neck"
partway through, with the neck-to-pocket transition rounded by a true
measured r10 fillet on each side (the "easy to grab" access notch).

## Decomposition

`introspect.py` triage: 1 solid, `reconstruct_coverage_pct=100`,
`freeform_area_pct=0`, `structural_freeform_pct=0`, `wrap_regions=0`,
`boolean_risk=low` (0 bspline faces), `solidity.verdict="hollow-or-lattice"`
(fill_ratio 0.314 — correctly flags a thin-walled hollow bin, not a solid
block). `face_count_by_type`: 7004 plane + 14 cylinder, **zero** cone/
sphere/torus/bspline. The part is nominally 100% analytic.

**The 7004-plane count is a tessellation artifact, not freeform geometry**
(see pitfalls.md item 2 for the full story): almost every curved transition
in the source except the 14 true cylindrical faces is represented as
thousands of tiny (<2mm²) flat facets rather than a genuine analytic
surface — consistent with an OpenSCAD-style or mesh-repaired export. This
recovery reconstructs the *intended* analytic geometry (true circular
fillets where they're real, flat walls/floor elsewhere), not the facet
noise; `topology_audit.py`'s face census confirms the rebuild's face count
is far *lower* than the source almost everywhere (the safe direction —
fewer redundant seams, 0 vs 4435).

Construction, in `main.py`:

1. **Outer envelope** (`_outer_envelope`) — a standard Gridfinity foot
   (3-band taper: chamfer/straight/chamfer, corner radius shrinking with
   the footprint, same technique as the `gridfinity-plain-bin` sibling
   recovery) fused to a full-length TALL rounded-rect prism. A **periodic
   foot-notch cutter** (`_cut_foot_notches`) then removes 2 small 6.5x6.5mm
   relief notches per grid unit on each rail (standard Gridfinity
   baseplate-interlock relief — see pitfalls.md item 4, found via the
   fidelity gate's own overshoot localizer).
2. **Roof cut** (`_roof_cut_tool`) — a Y-axis ruled loft through 18 measured
   `(Y, top_z)` breakpoints removes the top of the handle zone down to
   `short_top_z`, reproducing the source's stepped outer height. This is
   the recovery's main *approximation*: the true transition is closer to a
   compound multi-radius fillet than a piecewise-linear loft can capture —
   see "Gate results" and pitfalls.md item 7 for the honest cost of that.
3. **Interior cavity** (`_cavity_solid`) — one polygon (3 rectangles: wide
   handle pocket + narrow waist + wide business pocket) extruded from the
   flat floor (`floor_z`) past the top, with the 4 waist-to-wide reflex
   corners cut by **solid r10 cylinders centred at the source's own
   measured fillet-face axis locations** (not a vertex/edge `.fillet()` —
   see pitfalls.md item 3 for why that goes the wrong direction on this
   tool). The two Y end-caps get a small corner-radius `.fillet()` (this
   direction *is* correct — see the same pitfalls.md entry for the
   distinction).

There is **no wrap / no residual sidecar** — `wrap_regions=0` at triage and
the scaffold is 100% live parametric code end to end. `parts/residual_0.step`
is an empty placeholder, present only because `residual_audit.py`'s CLI
requires a positional residual path (same convention as the plain-bin
sibling).

## Gate results

- **Fidelity** (`metrics.py`, single solid) — **FAIL** (deliberate, documented
  waiver — see below). Volume error **0.025%**, centre-of-mass error
  **0.19%** — both comfortably inside the 0.5% tolerance. Principal-moments
  error **2.40%** exceeds it (worst axis governs the verdict). Overshoot is
  effectively zero (0.0024% of original volume, 3mm³ of facet-level noise) —
  the scaffold is a true subset of the original almost everywhere, so the
  remaining error is a *shape redistribution* inside the original's
  envelope (concentrated in the roof-transition zone), not missing or
  excess bulk. See "Iteration & the moments waiver" below.
- **Editability** (`perturb.py` / `check_liveness.py`, delta=0.1) — **PASS**.
  30/30 params LIVE, 0 DEAD, 0 ERROR (`cavity_end_fillet_r` was DEAD on an
  earlier iteration — unused because the actual end-cap radius is *derived*
  in `main.py` from `corner_radius` and the measured wall thickness at each
  rail, not driven directly — deleted per the skill's "delete dead knobs"
  hygiene rule; see `editability_gate.json`).
- **Reconstructability** (`residual_audit.py`) — **PASS** trivially:
  `punted_fraction = 0.0` (there is no residual to audit).
- **Topology** (`topology_audit.py`) — **FAIL** on `surface_area` (2.67% vs
  0.5% tolerance — the same underlying roof-transition-zone approximation
  driving the moments error). `redundant_seams` **PASS** (0 in the rebuild
  vs 4435 in the source — the rebuild never over-splits a face; all of the
  source's redundancy is the tessellation artifact described above). The
  face-census advisory shows large negative deltas almost everywhere
  (fewer faces per bucket than the source — expected/rewarded, matches the
  facet-collapse) and a handful of small positive deltas confined to the
  roof-ramp's own angled loft facets (10 faces at most per bucket) — the
  same feature, not a new one.

### Iteration & the moments/surface-area waiver

Both remaining FAILs trace to the same root cause: the roof-transition zone
(`roof_ramp`, Y from -38 to -29) is reconstructed as an 18-breakpoint
piecewise-linear ruled loft, an approximation of what is very likely a
compound small-radius fillet blend in the source (confirmed indirectly: the
source carries small r5 cylindrical "shoulder" fillets flanking the r10
main transition — see pitfalls.md item 3 — that this recovery did not
reconstruct). After ~10 tuning iterations (well past the skill's "soft cap
~4, then decide" guidance — see the Timeline) — including doubling the
ramp's breakpoint density, capping the r10 boss cut's Z-extent to the
source's measured `[1.64, 39.64]` band, and a numeric sweep of
`cavity_x_lo/hi` and `floor_z` against the volume metric — the moments
error plateaued around 2.4-2.8% with diminishing returns per iteration.

**The skill's normal escape hatch (take the residual, `fast_union` it back)
is not usable here**: `fast_cut(original, scaffold)` returns a spurious
~100%-of-part "missing" lump (125,142mm³ out of 125,184mm³) rather than the
true small delta — a boolean degeneracy from the huge number of coincident
tessellated facets in the source (the same 7004-plane artifact) confusing
the fuzzy solver. Cross-checked against the boolean-free `metrics.py`
volume comparison (scaffold 125,215mm³ vs original 125,184mm³, a 0.025%
difference) to confirm the fast_cut result is wrong, not the scaffold. When
actually unioned despite this, fidelity got *worse* (volume error jumped to
2.44%), confirming the residual step is actively harmful on this part, not
merely unnecessary. See pitfalls.md item 6.

Given this, the fidelity/topology FAILs on the moments/surface-area axes are
accepted as a **deliberate, documented gate waiver** — not a silent
slackening. The part is fully reconstructed as live parametric code (no
wrap), volume and COM are excellent, and the remaining error is a
sub-3%-of-inertia shape nuance in one ~9mm-long transition zone of a
209.5mm-long part.

## Editable parameters (`params.py`)

- **Footprint**: `grid_x` (1), `grid_y` (5), `unit_size` (42mm),
  `footprint_clearance` (0.5mm), `outer_center_x/y` (the source's off-origin
  footprint centre), `corner_radius` (4.0mm, circle-fit measured).
- **Bottom foot**: `foot_bottom_z`, `foot_taper1_h/delta`, `foot_straight_h`,
  `foot_taper2_h`, `foot_total_delta` — the 3-band taper.
- **Wall height (the stepped zones)**: `tall_top_z` (40.8), `short_top_z`
  (12.8), `roof_ramp` (18-breakpoint transition profile).
- **Interior cavity**: `floor_z`, `cavity_x_lo/hi` (wide pocket bounds),
  `waist_x_lo/hi`, `waist_y_lo/hi` (the neck), `transition_fillet_r` (10.0,
  measured exactly), `transition_fillet_z_hi` (39.64, where the fillet caps
  and the cavity returns to its un-bossed cross-section), `cavity_y_lo/hi`
  (channel end extents).
- **Foot-rail relief notches**: `notch_width` (6.5), `notch_rail_offset`
  (13.0), `notch_y_offsets` (4.5, 30.5 — the two per-unit positions),
  `notch_z_lo/hi`.

## Wrap honesty

There is no wrap. The whole part reconstructs as live parametric CadQuery
code; `gen_step()` never imports the original STEP. The `parts/residual_0.step`
sidecar is an empty placeholder (see "Decomposition" above).

## Assumptions

1. **No magnet/screw holes**, despite `design_readme.md`'s boilerplate
   "Has holes for magnets or screws" line — a direct face walk found all 14
   cylindrical faces are either the 4 r10 transition fillets or 10 r5 edge
   fillets; none are small (~3-6mm) holes anywhere on the bottom face. The
   readme text is very likely reused boilerplate from a sibling Gridfinity
   model in the same author's catalogue (the plain-bin/micro-SD-holder
   siblings in this repo both DO have magnet holes) rather than a
   description of this specific STEP.
2. **`outer_center_x`/`outer_center_y` (footprint centred at
   (-1.82, -12.36), not the origin)** is taken as-measured, not re-centred —
   preserves centre-of-mass alignment for the fidelity gate without needing
   a compensating translate.
3. **`roof_ramp`'s 18 breakpoints** are a *measured curve sample*, not a
   named/simplified feature — see "Iteration" above for why a cleaner
   closed-form (e.g. a single filleted step) was not achievable within the
   iteration budget. A future edit to `short_top_z`/`tall_top_z` moves the
   ramp's end heights correctly (LIVE per `perturb.py`) but does not
   re-derive the intermediate breakpoints' shape.
4. **Foot-rail notch positions** (`notch_y_offsets = (4.5, 30.5)`,
   `notch_rail_offset = 13.0`) were reverse-engineered from the fidelity
   gate's own overshoot-lump coordinates (see pitfalls.md item 4), not
   read directly off an analytic feature list — introspect.py's
   `planar_holes`/`lattices` did not surface them (they're on the
   *underside* of the foot, inside the tapered band, and are small).
